csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	RATIO_NOT_SCALED	note (what the tutor is told)	They typed the ratio of the two pressures as if it were the pressure asked for. A ratio has no unit; the unknown pressure is the known pressure times (or divided by) that ratio.

csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	SIGN_FLIPPED	ask (if no model, the question asked)	Does your answer make sense? More water presses down at depth. Should water boil at a higher or lower temperature there? The term you are isolating has a minus sign in front of it. Did you lose it?
csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	SIGN_FLIPPED	ask (if no model, the question asked)	Should your answer be higher or lower than the pressure you were given? Check your signs, and check which pressure goes with which temperature.
csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	SIGN_FLIPPED	ask (if no model, the question asked)	Think about the bird. The head is cooler than the body. Should the pressure in the head be higher or lower than in the body? Check your signs. Check which pressure goes with which temperature.
csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	SIGN_FLIPPED	ask (if no model, the question asked)	Think about the syringe. Pulling the plunger lowers the pressure. Should your answer be higher or lower than the pressure you were given? Check your signs. Check which pressure goes with which temperature.
csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	SIGN_FLIPPED	error id	SIGN_FLIPPED
csv	content_ch10_cc_errors.csv	SIGN_FLIPPED	note (what the tutor is told)	The answer sits on the wrong side of the known pressure: what a dropped minus sign gives, and crossing the pairs gives the same number. (P_WRONG_SIDE in the scenario's own terms = SIGN_FLIPPED on the pressure kinds.)

csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Deep-Sea Vent	Alt text for the images (read aloud by a screen reader)	Video, 21 seconds, with the sound of the vent. Deep-sea footage from a remotely operated vehicle. Pale mineral chimneys rise from the dark sea floor. Cloudy plumes pour from their tops and drift away. Close up, a thin white stream jets from an opening in the rock. Pale crabs and shrimp crowd the chimney walls. A caption reads MARIANIS TRENCH HYDROTHERMAL VENTS.
csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Deep-Sea Vent	Answer	Water molecules in the hot stream move fast, and many have enough energy to escape into vapor. But a bubble can only form if the vapor inside it pushes back as hard as the water pressing on it. Deep down, the weight of the water above squeezes the molecules together and keeps them from spreading apart into vapor. That pressure is very large, so the water must be much hotter before its vapor pressure is high enough to open a bubble. In shallower water the pressure is lower, so the water reaches its boiling point at a lower temperature, and it fizzes.
csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Deep-Sea Vent	CHAPTER 10	The Deep-Sea Vent
csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Deep-Sea Vent	Deterministic Keyword Check:	pressure, escape, energy, bubble, push, weight, squeeze, compress, restrain, external pressure, equilibrium vapor pressure, kinetic energy, overcome

csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Drinking Bird	Answer	Water molecules on the wet felt evaporate; the most energetic ones leave cooling the head. Inside the sealed glass, the cooler head has a lower vapor pressure than the warm body: vapor condenses on the cold glass, and fewer molecules escape from the cold liquid. The higher vapor pressure in the warm body pushes liquid up the neck. The top grows heavy and the bird tips.

csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Rubbing Alcohol Chill	Answer	Alcohol molecules have weaker intermolecular attractions than water, so a larger fraction of particles can overcome cohesive forces at room temperature to evaporate.

csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Syringe Boiling Trick	Alt text for the images (read aloud by a screen reader)	Two hands hold a large plastic syringe, tip up. The tip is sealed with a short rubber tube and a small capped plastic tube. A thin layer of water sits between the tip and the plunger's dark rubber seal. Small bubbles show in the water. csv	content_ch10_cc_openers.csv	The Syringe Boiling Trick	Answer	Water molecules at the surface keep escaping into vapor. At this temperature they have a set vapor pressure. Pulling the plunger lowers the external pressure on the water. When that external pressure falls to the water's vapor pressure, molecules inside the liquid can escape into bubbles of vapor, so the water boils. The molecules already have enough energy; no heat is added.

csv	content_ch13_eq_openers.csv	The Haber-Bosch Harvest	Answer	Nitrogen and hydrogen molecules collide and react to form ammonia. At the same time, ammonia molecules break apart into nitrogen and hydrogen again. As ammonia builds up, the reverse reaction speeds up and the forward reaction slows. When the two rates are equal, the amounts stop changing, but both reactions keep running. So some nitrogen and hydrogen are always left.
csv	content_ch13_eq_openers.csv	The Haber-Bosch Harvest	CHAPTER 13	The Haber-Bosch Harvest

csv	content_ch13_eq_openers.csv	The Martian Fuel Factory	Alt text for the images (read aloud by a screen reader)	A Mars landscape in rusty orange. csv	content_ch13_eq_openers.csv	The Martian Fuel Factory	Answer	Carbon monoxide and water molecules collide and swap atoms to make carbon dioxide and hydrogen. At the same time, carbon dioxide and hydrogen molecules collide and swap back. When the two happen at the same rate, the amounts stop changing, but both reactions keep running. Each swap trades two molecules for two, so the number of molecules, and the pressure, never changes.

csv	content_ch14_ph_openers.csv	Off the Scale	Alt text for the images (read aloud by a screen reader)	A stockroom bench at night. A pH meter's probe stands in a clear bottle labeled 2.0 M HCl; its screen reads −0.30. A second clear bottle labeled 2.5 M KOH stands beside it.
csv	content_ch14_ph_openers.csv	Off the Scale	Answer	HCl ionizes completely: H₃O⁺ + Cl⁻; [H₃O⁺] = 2.0 M > 1 M, so pH < 0. KOH dissociates completely: K⁺ + OH⁻; [OH⁻] = 2.5 M, so pOH < 0 and pH > 14. pH = −log[H₃O⁺] has no 0–14 bound.

csv	content_ch14_ph_openers.csv	The Corn Soak	Alt text for the images (read aloud by a screen reader)	A kitchen counter: a glass bowl of corn kernels soaking in milky-white water, a pH probe in the bowl, a meter reading 12.1, a bag labeled Pickling Lime (cal), and a stack of corn tortillas.
csv	content_ch14_ph_openers.csv	The Corn Soak	Answer	HCl transfers H⁺ to H₂O completely: H₃O⁺ + Cl⁻. Ca(OH)₂ dissociates completely: Ca²⁺ + 2OH⁻. Pure water autoionizes: [H₃O⁺] = [OH⁻] = 1.0 × 10⁻⁷ M. In all three, almost all H₂O stays molecular (about 55 M).
